SUFU

UniProt ID: Q9UMX1
Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback
143 Annotations 2 Core Functions 48 References 1 Documents 4 Questions 3 Experiments Raw YAML

Gene Description

SUFU (Suppressor of fused homolog) is a 484-residue intracellular protein with no catalytic domain and no enzymatic activity, and the single human member of the Suppressor of fused family. It is the principal negative regulator of the vertebrate Hedgehog pathway acting downstream of Smoothened. SUFU binds full-length GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 zinc-finger transcription factors through a conserved interface that closes around the SYGHL motif of GLI, and this single interaction accounts for its several documented effects: GLI is held in the cytoplasm and blocked from entering the nucleus, its transcriptional activity is repressed even where the two are bound together on DNA, and it is protected from proteasomal degradation. In the pathway-off state SUFU additionally acts as an adaptor that promotes proteolytic conversion of full-length GLI3 into the GLI3R repressor by recruiting GSK3B to GLI3; the SUFU-GLI3 complex is delivered to the primary cilium, and intraflagellar transport moves it between the ciliary base, where the processing kinases act, and the ciliary tip. Hedgehog signalling releases GLI from SUFU: the Fused-family kinases STK36 and ULK3, the ciliary kinase DYRK2, ERK/MAPK and S6K1 each weaken the SUFU-GLI interface by phosphorylating GLI, and SCF(FBXL17) ubiquitylates SUFU itself at Lys-257 to drive its turnover in the nucleus. SUFU is expressed ubiquitously in adult tissues and is found in the cytoplasm, the nucleoplasm and the cilium. Loss of SUFU raises Hedgehog output: knockout is embryonic-lethal in mouse, germline loss-of-function in humans predisposes to medulloblastoma and causes basal cell nevus syndrome, and hypomorphic recessive missense variants cause Joubert syndrome with cranio-facial and skeletal defects.

Existing Annotations Review

GO Term Evidence Action Reason
GO:0000122 negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
IDA
PMID:10564661
Characterization of the human suppressor of fused, a negativ... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Stone et al. showed that human SUFU represses the transcriptional activity of GLI in reporter assays while binding GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 directly. Because SUFU acts on the transcription factor itself rather than on an upstream input, repression of GLI target genes is an immediate consequence of SUFU binding.
Reason: Accepted as a core process. GLI1/2/3 are RNA polymerase II transcription factors, and SUFU's only established biochemical activity is to bind them and hold them inactive, so this term names something SUFU genuinely executes rather than a distant downstream phenotype. The same term carries an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 in the local PAINT slice, placing the repression function deep in Eumetazoa. (The human GOA snapshot does not yet carry a matching IBA row for GO:0000122; that is snapshot lag between the PAINT slice and the GOA release, not a disagreement, and it changes nothing here.)
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10564661
HSu(fu) was found to repress activity of the zinc-finger transcription factor Gli, which mediates Hedgehog signaling in vertebrates, and to physically interact with Gli, Gli2 and Gli3
PMID:10564661
our data provide biochemical and functional evidence for the hypothesis that Su(fu) is a key negative regulator in the vertebrate Hedgehog signaling pathway
GO:0000122 negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
IEA
GO_REF:0000117 		ACCEPT
Summary: An ARBA machine-learning rule (ARBA00029133) assigns negative regulation of Pol II transcription to SUFU on the basis of its UniProt family and functional annotation.
Reason: Accepted. The electronic assignment lands on exactly the term that the human IDA row and the PAINT node independently support, so the rule is correct for this protein rather than being a broad default. Where an ARBA rule is too generic for SUFU it has been modified instead (see GO:0007165), so this is not blanket acceptance of the ARBA source.

Rule output coincides with the human IDA annotation and with the PTHR10928 IBD node, so the electronic call is independently corroborated.

GO:0000122 negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
TAS
PMID:11001584
The sonic hedgehog-patched-gli pathway in human development ... 		ACCEPT
Summary: A GOA row taken from the Villavicencio et al. review of the sonic hedgehog-patched-gli pathway in human development and disease, asserting that SUFU negatively regulates GLI-driven transcription.
Reason: Accepted. The cached record for this review contains citation metadata only, with no abstract or full text, so the author statement behind the TAS cannot be quoted here and no supporting_text is offered. The assertion itself is not in doubt: it duplicates a claim that the IDA row from PMID:10564661 establishes experimentally in human cells and that the ancestral PAINT node supports phylogenetically. A TAS row that restates a well-evidenced term is redundant rather than wrong, and UNDECIDED would misrepresent the state of the evidence for this term.
GO:0003714 transcription corepressor activity
TAS
PMID:10559945
Mammalian suppressor-of-fused modulates nuclear-cytoplasmic ... 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Kogerman et al. showed that SUFU sequesters GLI1 in the cytoplasm but can also interact with GLI1 while it is on DNA, which is the observation behind reading SUFU as a transcriptional corepressor rather than purely a cytoplasmic anchor.
Reason: Kept, but marked non-core. It is not wrong: SUFU is found with GLI1 on DNA, and UniProt records a DNA-bound corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3. But two things keep it off the core list. First, both GOA rows are TAS and the corepressor complex itself is annotated in UniProt only By similarity from mouse, so the human evidence is thinner than for sequestration. Second, and more decisively, the repression readout is already accounted for by the sequestering activity: Zhang et al. showed that mutating the single SUFU-GLI interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which means these are three readouts of one binding event rather than three activities. Promoting GO:0003714 to core would put a less-established mechanism on the same footing as the one that the PAINT node, the hedgehog_signaling module and all five curated GO-CAM models agree on.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10559945
the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1, can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
PMID:24217340
Mutations of critical interface residues disrupt the Sufu-Gli complex and prevent Sufu from repressing Gli-mediated transcription, tethering Gli in the cytoplasm and protecting Gli from the 26S proteasome-mediated degradation.
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3 (By similarity).
GO:0003714 transcription corepressor activity
TAS
PMID:10564661
Characterization of the human suppressor of fused, a negativ... 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Stone et al. reported that SUFU represses GLI-mediated transactivation while bound to GLI, and that repression is potentiated by co-expression of Slimb, consistent with SUFU acting on the transcription factor at the promoter as well as in the cytoplasm.
Reason: Kept, but marked non-core. It is not wrong: SUFU is found with GLI1 on DNA, and UniProt records a DNA-bound corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3. But two things keep it off the core list. First, both GOA rows are TAS and the corepressor complex itself is annotated in UniProt only By similarity from mouse, so the human evidence is thinner than for sequestration. Second, and more decisively, the repression readout is already accounted for by the sequestering activity: Zhang et al. showed that mutating the single SUFU-GLI interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which means these are three readouts of one binding event rather than three activities. Promoting GO:0003714 to core would put a less-established mechanism on the same footing as the one that the PAINT node, the hedgehog_signaling module and all five curated GO-CAM models agree on.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10564661
The data further suggest that Su(fu) can act by binding to Gli and inhibiting Gli-mediated transactivation as well as by serving as an adaptor protein, which links Gli to the Slimb-dependent proteasomal degradation pathway.
PMID:10564661
Coexpression of Slimb with Su(fu) potentiated the Su(fu)-mediated repression of Gli.

GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:10564661
Characterization of the human suppressor of fused, a negativ... 		MODIFY
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from the original characterisation of human SUFU, with GLI3 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface. The bare term records none of that.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term, so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.
Proposed replacements: DNA-binding transcription factor binding
Supporting Evidence:
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
transcription factors in the cytoplasm
GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:10806483
Gli regulation by the opposing activities of fused and suppr... 		MODIFY
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from the study of the opposing activities of Fused and Suppressor of fused with the Fused-family kinase STK36 as the partner. STK36 is the vertebrate orthologue of Drosophila Fused and acts on the SUFU-GLI complex to release GLI, so the interaction has a defined mechanistic role rather than being an isolated detection.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative, but here the partner class is itself the information: STK36 is a serine/threonine protein kinase and UniProt records the interaction together with its functional consequence. Protein kinase binding is the evidence-backed replacement, and SUFU already carries that term by IPI for the related kinase ULK3 (GO:0019901, PMID:20643644), so the replacement makes the record internally consistent rather than introducing a new claim.
Proposed replacements: protein kinase binding
Supporting Evidence:

PMID:16189514
Towards a proteome-scale map of the human protein-protein in... 		REMOVE
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from a proteome-scale yeast two-hybrid map; the reported partner is PEX26. The row asserts only that the two proteins were co-detected, and says nothing about what SUFU does.
Reason: Removed because the generic term carries no functional information, not because the measurement is doubted. PEX26 is not a documented participant in any SUFU mechanism recorded in the UniProt FUNCTION or SUBUNIT annotations, nor in the Hedgehog literature reviewed here, so there is no evidence-backed molecular function to promote the row to; inventing one from a single large-scale detection is exactly what the curation guidelines forbid. The interaction itself remains available in IntAct and in the UniProt INTERACTION block, where it is a better fit than a GO molecular-function assertion.

SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface. Parallel SCF adaptor capture proteomics reveals a role for S... 		REMOVE
Summary: GOA records this row from a parallel SCF-adaptor capture proteomics screen, with FBXL17 as the partner. That study is a systematic survey of F-box protein interactors whose validation went to BACH1 and the NRF2 axis; SUFU appears as one candidate among many.
Reason: Removed as uninformative. The SUFU-FBXL17 relationship is real and is retained through the companion row anchored to PMID:27234298, which established it mechanistically and is modified here to ubiquitin protein ligase binding. This screening row would add a second bare protein-binding assertion for the same pair without adding functional content.

A proteome-scale map of the human interactome network. 		REMOVE
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from a proteome-scale binary interactome map; the reported partner is ZNF747. The row asserts only that the two proteins were co-detected, and says nothing about what SUFU does.
Reason: Removed because the generic term carries no functional information, not because the measurement is doubted. ZNF747 is not a documented participant in any SUFU mechanism recorded in the UniProt FUNCTION or SUBUNIT annotations, nor in the Hedgehog literature reviewed here, so there is no evidence-backed molecular function to promote the row to; inventing one from a single large-scale detection is exactly what the curation guidelines forbid. The interaction itself remains available in IntAct and in the UniProt INTERACTION block, where it is a better fit than a GO molecular-function assertion.

PMID:25609649
Proteomic analyses reveal distinct chromatin-associated and ... 		MODIFY
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from a proteomic survey of chromatin-associated and soluble transcription factor complexes, with GLI1 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface. The bare term records none of that.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term, so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.
Proposed replacements: DNA-binding transcription factor binding

PMID:27234298
SCF (Fbxl17) ubiquitylation of Sufu regulates Hedgehog signa... 		MODIFY
Summary: This row is anchored to the mechanistic study of SCF(FBXL17), which shows that FBXL17 - the substrate-recognition F-box subunit of an SCF ubiquitin ligase - engages SUFU and targets it for proteolysis in the nucleus, thereby releasing GLI1.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative, and here a specific, evidence-backed term is available: SUFU is the substrate that engages an SCF E3 ubiquitin ligase complex, which GO expresses as ubiquitin protein ligase binding. UniProt localises the resulting modification to Lys-257, so the engagement is documented at residue level rather than inferred from co-purification. Note the direction of the claim - this term says SUFU binds the ligase, not that SUFU ubiquitylates anything.
Proposed replacements: ubiquitin protein ligase binding
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:27234298
Here, we show that Fbxl17 (F-box and leucine-rich repeat protein 17) targets Sufu for proteolysis in the nucleus.
PMID:27234298
The ubiquitylation of Sufu, mediated by Fbxl17, allows the release of Gli1 from Sufu for proper Hh signal transduction.

SLC41A3 is not a documented participant in any SUFU mechanism recorded in the UniProt FUNCTION or SUBUNIT annotations, nor in the Hedgehog literature reviewed here, so there is no evidence-backed molecular function to promote the row to; inventing one from a single large-scale detection is exactly what the curation guidelines forbid. The interaction itself remains available in IntAct and in the UniProt INTERACTION block, where it is a better fit than a GO molecular-function assertion.

a bare `protein binding` row
GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:35140242
Human transcription factor protein interaction networks. 		MODIFY
Summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from a human transcription-factor interaction network, with GLI3 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface. The bare term records none of that.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term, so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.
Proposed replacements: DNA-binding transcription factor binding
Supporting Evidence:
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
transcription factors in the cytoplasm
GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:35512704
Systematic discovery of mutation-directed neo-protein-protei... 		REMOVE
Summary: GOA records this row from a screen for mutation-directed neo-interactions in cancer, with BRAF as the reported partner. That dataset is designed to surface interactions gained by mutant proteins rather than interactions of the wild-type pair.
Reason: Removed as uninformative, and the experimental design additionally blocks promotion to a specific term. A neo-interaction involving a mutant kinase is not evidence that wild-type SUFU has protein kinase binding activity toward BRAF, so unlike the STK36 and ULK3 rows this one cannot be rescued by a more informative molecular function. No SUFU-BRAF relationship appears in the UniProt FUNCTION or SUBUNIT annotations.

GO:0005634 nucleus
EXP
PMID:10559945
Mammalian suppressor-of-fused modulates nuclear-cytoplasmic ... 		ACCEPT
Summary: The original human SUFU paper localised the protein and showed that it modulates the nuclear-cytoplasmic distribution of GLI1.
Reason: Accepted as a functionally relevant location rather than as incidental presence. The nuclear pool does work: SUFU engages GLI1 on DNA (PMID:10559945), and SCF(FBXL17) degrades SUFU specifically in the nucleus to release GLI1 (PMID:27234298), so nuclear SUFU is both an actor and a regulated target there. The shuttling behaviour is the mechanism by which SUFU controls where GLI is allowed to accumulate.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10559945
may affect nuclear-cytoplasmic shuttling of GLI-1 or the activity of GLI-1 in the nucleus
PMID:10559945
the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1, can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
GO:0005634 nucleus
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Phylogenetic inference from PTHR10928 node PTN000859153, which carries an IBD for nucleus seeded by Drosophila Su(fu), mouse Sufu and human SUFU.
Reason: Accepted. This is a node assertion, not a similarity transfer: the PAINT curator judged that nuclear localisation is ancestral within the Suppressor of fused family and human SUFU descends from that node. Human SUFU appears in the WITH/FROM because its own experimental localisation is one of the descendant evidences used to place the IBD - that is the expected pattern for a gene with direct evidence, and the IBA then adds the further claim that the localisation is inherited rather than vertebrate-specific. Independent human experimental rows (EXP, IDA, IMP) agree.
Propagation Review
Root cause: NO FAILURE CORE
Sources checked:
PANTHER:PTN000859153 · PTN000859153 SUPPORTS TRANSFER
PTHR10928 node PTN000859153, placed at taxon:6072 with IBD support seeded by Drosophila Su(fu) (FB:FBgn0005355), mouse Sufu (MGI:MGI:1345643) and human SUFU. Human SUFU appearing among the seeds is expected, not circular: its own experimental annotations are part of the descendant evidence the PAINT curator used to place the node.

GO_REF:0000120 		ACCEPT

Hypomorphic Recessive Variants in SUFU Impair the Sonic Hedg... 		ACCEPT
Summary: De Mori et al. examined SUFU localisation in patient fibroblasts and cellular models carrying hypomorphic SUFU missense variants.
Reason: Accepted as a functionally relevant location rather than as incidental presence. The nuclear pool does work: SUFU engages GLI1 on DNA (PMID:10559945), and SCF(FBXL17) degrades SUFU specifically in the nucleus to release GLI1 (PMID:27234298), so nuclear SUFU is both an actor and a regulated target there. GO:0005654 nucleoplasm
IDA
GO_REF:0000052 		ACCEPT
Summary: An immunofluorescence-based localisation (GO_REF:0000052, the Human Protein Atlas curation stream) placing SUFU in the nucleoplasm.
Reason: Accepted. Nucleoplasm is the specific sub-compartment of the nuclear pool that the other five nucleus rows report more coarsely, and it is where a soluble, non-chromatin-bound regulator of a transcription factor would be expected. It adds granularity without asserting anything the nucleus rows do not.
GO:0005737 cytoplasm
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Phylogenetic inference from PTHR10928 node PTN000859153, which carries an IBD for cytoplasm seeded by Drosophila Su(fu), mouse Sufu and human SUFU.
Reason: Accepted as the primary site of the core activity. Sequestration is definitionally a statement about location: SUFU holds GLI1/2/3 in the cytoplasm and thereby blocks their nuclear translocation, so the cytoplasmic pool is where the molecular function is executed. All five curated GO-CAM models that contain SUFU place its protein sequestering activity in the cytoplasm, and the PTHR10928 node carries an IBD for this term. The IBA is a node assertion rather than a pairwise transfer, and the appearance of human SUFU in its own WITH/FROM reflects that its experimental localisation helped place the node.

GO:0005929 cilium
IEA
GO_REF:0000120 		ACCEPT
Summary: Combined automated annotation (GO_REF:0000120) assigning cilium, transferred from the mouse orthologue Sufu (Q9Z0P7) together with the UniProt subcellular-location vocabulary.
Reason: Accepted. UniProt records the ciliary location for human SUFU only By similarity, but the compartment is mechanistically load-bearing rather than incidental in the vertebrate implementation of this pathway: the SUFU-GLI3 complex is recruited to cilia in the pathway-off state, which is where efficient GLI3 processing occurs, and Reactome curates the intraflagellar transport of GLI:SUFU between ciliary base and tip as explicit reactions. Independent work on DYRK2 reports abnormal SUFU localisation in Dyrk2-null cells while SMO localisation is unaffected, which is hard to explain unless SUFU is normally a ciliary resident. Accepting a By-similarity compartment is defensible here because three independent curation streams - UniProt, Reactome and the GO-CAM models - place SUFU there.

GO:0006355 regulation of DNA-templated transcription
IDA
PMID:24311597
Structural basis of SUFU-GLI interaction in human Hedgehog s... 		MODIFY
Summary: Cherry et al. measured GLI1-driven reporter transcription in HEK 293 cells transfected with wild-type and interface-mutant SUFU constructs, showing that SUFU represses GLI activity and that specific interface residues are required for it.
Reason: The essence is right but the term is the uninformative parent. SUFU regulates transcription in one direction only - it represses - and only at RNA polymerase II promoters driven by GLI transcription factors. There is no evidence anywhere in this record for SUFU raising transcription of anything, so the neutral parent term understates what is known. The child term proposed here is already carried by SUFU with IDA, IEA and TAS evidence and is supported by an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node, so this is a demotion of a redundant parent rather than a new claim.
Proposed replacements: negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II

GO:0007165 signal transduction
IEA
GO_REF:0000117 		MODIFY
Summary: An ARBA machine-learning rule (ARBA00029050) assigning the top-level signal transduction term to SUFU.
Reason: The essence is right but the term is so general that it conveys nothing about this protein. SUFU is not a generic signal transducer: its entire documented role is one specific negative step in one specific pathway, and GOA already carries that precise term eight times with IBA, IDA, IEA, IMP and TAS evidence. The guideline that an IEA may legitimately be broader than the experimental picture does not stretch to a term this far up the hierarchy, because 'signal transduction' would be equally true of several thousand human proteins with no shared biology. Modifying rather than removing preserves the intent of the original assignment.

GO:0007286 spermatid development
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) of spermatid development onto human SUFU from the rat orthologue Sufu (UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606).
Reason: Not absurd, but over-annotated. Tracing the donor shows that the rat annotation is an IEP - inferred from expression pattern - made from PMID:21893610, 'Hedgehog signalling promotes germ cell survival in the rat testis'. Hedgehog signalling genuinely operates in the testis, so a SUFU role there is plausible, which is why this is flagged rather than removed. But an expression correlation in rat establishes only that the gene is present during spermatogenesis; it does not show that SUFU executes a step of spermatid development, and the inference then travels a second time, electronically, into human. Two stacked weak inferences should not produce a tissue-specific developmental process annotation on a human gene. No human or mouse experimental evidence for this term appears anywhere in the record.

GO:0010954 positive regulation of protein processing
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		ACCEPT
Summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) from mouse Sufu of a role in promoting protein processing. The referent is GLI3: in the pathway-off state the SUFU-GLI3 complex is recruited to cilia and SUFU promotes the recruitment of GSK3B to GLI3, driving efficient proteolytic conversion of full-length GLI3 into the repressor form GLI3R.
Reason: Accepted as core, and it passes the participation test explicitly. SUFU is not merely required for GLI3 processing and is not the substrate of it - GLI3 is. SUFU does part of the work: it holds GLI3 and recruits the kinase that marks it, which is the scaffolding contribution that GO counts as participation. The human evidence is direct rather than purely orthologous: De Mori et al. showed that hypomorphic human SUFU variants reduce the capacity to bind GLI3 and promote its cleavage into GLI3R, which is a loss-of-function test of exactly this claim in human cells. This is also the one SUFU activity that is genuinely separable from sequestration, which is why it is given its own core function below.

GO:0030674 protein-macromolecule adaptor activity
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		ACCEPT
Summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) from mouse Sufu of adaptor activity. The underlying biology is that SUFU physically bridges GLI to the enzymes that act on it - GSK3B for GLI3 processing, and the Slimb/BTRC-dependent degradation machinery.
Reason: Accepted as core. This is the term the curation guidelines point to as the informative alternative to generic protein binding, and it is the one molecular function that captures what SUFU does beyond holding GLI still. The claim is not only orthologous: Stone et al. proposed the adaptor reading from human data in the original characterisation, describing SUFU as linking Gli to the Slimb-dependent proteasomal degradation pathway, and UniProt records the GSK3B-recruitment mechanism for GLI3 processing. Together with GO:0010954 this forms the second of the two core functions.

GO:0042308 negative regulation of protein import into nucleus
TAS
PMID:11001584
The sonic hedgehog-patched-gli pathway in human development ... 		ACCEPT
Summary: A GOA row from the Villavicencio et al. review, asserting that SUFU negatively regulates nuclear import. The referent is GLI: SUFU holds GLI1/2/3 in the cytoplasm and thereby blocks their translocation into the nucleus.
Reason: Accepted. This is the process-level statement of the sequestering activity, and it is directly evidenced rather than inferred: Yoshida et al. state plainly that SUFU inhibits Hedgehog signalling by retaining GLI2 and GLI3 in the cytoplasm and blocking their nuclear translocation, and the same paper shows that relieving the interaction by phosphorylation permits translocation. A negative-regulation term is the correct construction here: nuclear import is performed by the import machinery, and SUFU regulates whether GLI is available to it. The cached record for the cited review is citation metadata only, so the supporting text is taken from the primary literature and attributed to it.

The sonic hedgehog-patched-gli pathway in human development ... 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: A GOA row from the Villavicencio et al. review. The underlying experimental observation is in Kogerman et al., who showed that SUFU inhibits osteogenic differentiation in response to Sonic hedgehog signalling; UniProt additionally notes SUFU expression in osteoblasts of the perichondrium in the developing limb.
Reason: Kept, but non-core. The observation is real and is human, so removal would be wrong. It is nevertheless a downstream tissue-level consequence of SUFU restraining GLI activity in a particular cell type, not a distinct thing SUFU does: the molecular event is the same GLI-sequestering step already annotated, read out through an osteogenic differentiation assay. SUFU has no osteoblast-specific mechanism and no bone-restricted partner in the record. This is the standard treatment for the developmental outputs of a pleiotropic signalling regulator.

GO:0045879 negative regulation of smoothened signaling pathway
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Phylogenetic inference from PTHR10928 node PTN000859153, with IBD support seeded by Drosophila Su(fu) (FB:FBgn0005355), mouse Sufu (MGI:MGI:1345643), zebrafish sufu and human SUFU.
Reason: Accepted as the core biological process. This is the term that every independent line of evidence converges on: eight GOA rows spanning IBA, IDA, IEA, IMP and TAS; an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 seeded by fly, mouse, zebrafish and human; the sufu_sequestration annoton of the hedgehog_signaling module; and all five curated human GO-CAM models that contain SUFU. Nothing in the literature reviewed here contradicts it. As a node assertion this IBA says something the experimental rows do not: that negative regulation of Smoothened signalling is ancestral within the family rather than a vertebrate innovation. That is a meaningful additional claim given that the fly protein is dispensable for embryogenesis while the mammalian one is essential - the difference is in phenotypic weight, not in mechanism. Human SUFU in the WITH/FROM is expected: its own experimental annotations are among the descendant evidences behind the IBD.

GO_REF:0000120 		ACCEPT
Summary: Combined automated annotation (GO_REF:0000120) assigning negative regulation of the smoothened signaling pathway, drawing on an ARBA rule and the mouse orthologue Sufu.
Reason: Accepted as the core biological process. This is the term that every independent line of evidence converges on: eight GOA rows spanning IBA, IDA, IEA, IMP and TAS; an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 seeded by fly, mouse, zebrafish and human; the sufu_sequestration annoton of the hedgehog_signaling module; and all five curated human GO-CAM models that contain SUFU. Nothing in the literature reviewed here contradicts it. The electronic call reproduces a term with five independent experimental rows on the human protein, so it carries no inferential risk.

PMID:27234298
SCF (Fbxl17) ubiquitylation of Sufu regulates Hedgehog signa... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Raducu et al. showed that FBXL17-mediated ubiquitylation of SUFU releases GLI1 for signalling, that depleting FBXL17 gives defective Hedgehog signalling with impaired proliferation and medulloblastoma growth, and that a Gorlin-syndrome SUFU mutation which accelerates SUFU turnover produces sustained pathway activation - loss of SUFU raising pathway output is the defining behaviour of a negative regulator.
Reason: Accepted as the core biological process. This is the term that every independent line of evidence converges on: eight GOA rows spanning IBA, IDA, IEA, IMP and TAS; an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 seeded by fly, mouse, zebrafish and human; the sufu_sequestration annoton of the hedgehog_signaling module; and all five curated human GO-CAM models that contain SUFU. Nothing in the literature reviewed here contradicts it.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:27234298
Sufu (Suppressor of fused) is a central regulator of Hh (Hedgehog) signaling and acts as a tumor suppressor by maintaining the Gli (Glioma-associated oncogene homolog) transcription factors inactive.
PMID:27234298
Depletion of Fbxl17 leads to defective Hh signaling associated with an impaired cancer cell proliferation and medulloblastoma tumor growth.
PMID:27234298
we identify a mutation in Sufu, occurring in medulloblastoma of patients with Gorlin syndrome, which increases Sufu turnover through Fbxl17-mediated polyubiquitylation and leads to a sustained Hh signaling activation

PMID:28965847
Hypomorphic Recessive Variants in SUFU Impair the Sonic Hedg... 		ACCEPT
Summary: De Mori et al. identified homozygous hypomorphic SUFU missense variants in children with Joubert syndrome, showed that the variants reduce SUFU stability and GLI3 binding, and demonstrated the resulting loss of SHH-pathway repression by target-gene expression.
Reason: Accepted as the core biological process. This is the term that every independent line of evidence converges on: eight GOA rows spanning IBA, IDA, IEA, IMP and TAS; an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 seeded by fly, mouse, zebrafish and human; the sufu_sequestration annoton of the hedgehog_signaling module; and all five curated human GO-CAM models that contain SUFU. Nothing in the literature reviewed here contradicts it. This is the strongest human genetic evidence in the record: germline loss-of-function in the negative regulator produces a phenotype with both SHH-pathway and ciliopathy features.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:28965847
SUFU is the main negative regulator of the SHH pathway and is essential during development.
PMID:28965847
Functional studies on cellular models and fibroblasts showed that both variants significantly reduced SUFU stability and its capacity to bind GLI3 and promote its cleavage into the repressor form GLI3R.
PMID:28965847
In turn, this impaired SUFU-mediated repression of the SHH pathway, as shown by altered expression levels of several target genes.

GO:0097542 ciliary tip
TAS
Reactome:R-HSA-5610766 		ACCEPT
Summary: Reactome places SUFU at the ciliary tip as a participant in the curated reaction "The intraflagellar transport A (IFT-A) complex is required for the transit of GLI:SUFU complexes to the ciliary base".
Reason: Accepted. In the vertebrate implementation of this pathway the GLI:SUFU complex is carried by anterograde intraflagellar transport to the ciliary tip on pathway activation, and it is there that the complex disassembles and GLI is released. Reactome curates these as explicit reactions with SUFU as a participant, and UniProt records that SUFU is recruited to cilia in response to Smoothened signalling, leading to dissociation from GLI. The location is therefore where a discrete step of the core mechanism happens, not incidental presence.

GO:0097546 ciliary base
TAS
Reactome:R-HSA-5610720 		ACCEPT
Summary: Reactome places SUFU at the ciliary base as a participant in the curated reaction "PKA phosphorylates GLI3".
Reason: Accepted. The ciliary base is where the pathway-off processing cassette operates on the GLI:SUFU complex: retrograde IFT-A transport returns the complex there, the PKA/CK1/GSK3 phosphorylation and beta-TrCP-dependent partial proteolysis that generate GLI3R are curated at that location, and SUFU is a participant in each of those reactions because it is what holds GLI while the kinases act. Independent work places the SMO-dependent DYRK2 phosphorylation of GLI2/GLI3 at the ciliary base as well. This is the compartment in which SUFU's adaptor contribution to GLI3 processing is executed.
Supporting Evidence:
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
promoting recruitment of GSK3B to GLI3
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
leading to the efficient processing of full-length GLI3 into

GO:0097730 non-motile cilium
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		ACCEPT
Summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) of non-motile cilium onto human SUFU from the mouse orthologue Sufu (Q9Z0P7).
Reason: Accepted. This is the correct refinement of the parent cilium annotation: Hedgehog signalling in vertebrates runs through the primary cilium, which is non-motile, and the SUFU-relevant events - IFT transport of GLI:SUFU, ciliary-base GLI3 processing, tip dissociation - are all primary-cilium biology. The SUFU-related ciliopathy phenotype (Joubert syndrome with the molar tooth sign) is itself a primary-cilium disorder, which is independent human support for the compartment.
Propagation Review
Root cause: NO FAILURE NON CORE
Sources checked:
UniProtKB:Q9Z0P7 · Sufu SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Mouse Sufu is the donor; the refinement from cilium to non-motile cilium is consistent with the vertebrate primary-cilium implementation of the pathway in both species.

GO:0140311 protein sequestering activity
IDA
PMID:10559945
Mammalian suppressor-of-fused modulates nuclear-cytoplasmic ... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Kogerman et al. showed that human SUFU binds GLI1 and can sequester it in the cytoplasm, the observation that named the activity.
Reason: Accepted as the core molecular function. SUFU has no catalytic domain and no enzymatic activity; what it does is bind full-length GLI1/GLI2/GLI3 through a defined interface and hold them out of productive service. Zhang et al. showed that disrupting that single interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which establishes that these are three readouts of one sequestering event rather than three separate activities. The term carries an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node, is the molecular function of the sufu_sequestration annoton in the hedgehog_signaling module, and is the molecular function assigned to SUFU in all five curated human GO-CAM models.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:10559945
the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1, can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
PMID:24217340
Mutations of critical interface residues disrupt the Sufu-Gli complex and prevent Sufu from repressing Gli-mediated transcription, tethering Gli in the cytoplasm and protecting Gli from the 26S proteasome-mediated degradation.
GO:0140311 protein sequestering activity
IDA
PMID:24311597
Structural basis of SUFU-GLI interaction in human Hedgehog s... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Cherry et al. determined the crystal and SAXS structures of full-length human SUFU alone and bound to the conserved GLI SYGHL motif, showing that GLI binding drives a large conformational change in which the N- and C-terminal domains clamp the GLI peptide.
Reason: Accepted as the core molecular function. SUFU has no catalytic domain and no enzymatic activity; what it does is bind full-length GLI1/GLI2/GLI3 through a defined interface and hold them out of productive service. Zhang et al. showed that disrupting that single interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which establishes that these are three readouts of one sequestering event rather than three separate activities. The term carries an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node, is the molecular function of the sufu_sequestration annoton in the hedgehog_signaling module, and is the molecular function assigned to SUFU in all five curated human GO-CAM models. This row supplies the structural basis: sequestration here is a physical clamp around a short conserved motif, not a vague association.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:24311597
SUFU is an essential intracellular negative regulator of mammalian Hedgehog signalling and acts by binding and modulating the activity of GLI transcription factors.
PMID:24311597
Upon GLI binding, SUFU undergoes a large conformational change in which the N- and C-terminal domains come together to clamp highly conserved GLI residues
PMID:24311597
the minimal SUFU binding motif in GLI encompasses the amino acids SYGHL

IDA
PMID:35831023
ERK2 MAP kinase regulates SUFU binding by multisite phosphor... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Bardwell et al. measured the affinity of the GLI1-SUFU interaction directly and showed that ERK2 phosphorylation of three conserved GLI1 serines weakens it by over 25-fold, with a corresponding increase in GLI1-driven transcription.
Reason: Accepted as the core molecular function. SUFU has no catalytic domain and no enzymatic activity; what it does is bind full-length GLI1/GLI2/GLI3 through a defined interface and hold them out of productive service. Zhang et al. showed that disrupting that single interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which establishes that these are three readouts of one sequestering event rather than three separate activities. The term carries an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node, is the molecular function of the sufu_sequestration annoton in the hedgehog_signaling module, and is the molecular function assigned to SUFU in all five curated human GO-CAM models. Quantifying how much the interaction must be weakened to release GLI is a direct measurement of the sequestering activity itself.

GO:1990788 GLI-SUFU complex
IPI
PMID:24311597
Structural basis of SUFU-GLI interaction in human Hedgehog s... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Cherry et al. reconstituted and crystallised the human SUFU-GLI complex, defining the interface and the conserved SYGHL motif in GLI that SUFU clamps.
Reason: Accepted. The GLI-SUFU complex is not an incidental co-purification but the physical entity through which SUFU acts: it is what Reactome transports between ciliary base and tip, what the kinases disassemble, and what the GO-CAM models instantiate. Two independent co-structures define it. Recording SUFU as part_of this complex is the cellular-component counterpart of its protein sequestering activity.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:24311597
Upon GLI binding, SUFU undergoes a large conformational change in which the N- and C-terminal domains come together to clamp highly conserved GLI residues
PMID:24311597
the minimal SUFU binding motif in GLI encompasses the amino acids SYGHL
PMID:24217340
reveal that Sufu alternates between 'open' and 'closed' conformations

Summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) onto human SUFU from the rat orthologue Sufu (UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606).
Reason: Removed. Tracing the donor shows the rat annotation is an IEP - inferred from expression pattern - made from PMID:30790292, a study reporting that Oroxylin A increases the sensitivity of glioma cells to temozolomide via an HIF-1alpha/Hedgehog axis under hypoxia. That is a cell-line pharmacology observation, not a physiological function, and it has been propagated twice: first into a process annotation on the rat gene on expression evidence alone, then electronically into human. Nothing in the human record - no experimental row, no PAINT node term, no GO-CAM model, no UniProt statement - supports human SUFU having a role in drug response. This is distinct from the spermatid-development row, which is flagged rather than removed because a testis role is at least biologically coherent for a Hedgehog regulator; a positive role in cellular drug response is not a function this protein can be said to have.
Propagation Review
Root cause: SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
Failure modes: SOURCE EVIDENCE WEAK CONTEXT OR TISSUE MISMATCH
Sources checked:
UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606 · Sufu SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
Rat donor annotation is IEP from PMID:30790292, a glioma chemosensitisation study; an expression change under drug treatment does not establish a regulatory function.
Supporting Evidence:
file:human/SUFU/SUFU-notes.md
Tracing the donor annotations in QuickGO shows both rest on **IEP**

Core Functions

Sequestration of the GLI transcription factors. SUFU binds full-length GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 through a bipartite interface that clamps the conserved SYGHL motif, holding them in the cytoplasm, blocking their nuclear import, repressing their transcriptional output and shielding them from proteasomal degradation. These are readouts of one binding event: mutating the interface abolishes all of them together. The activity is relieved when GLI is phosphorylated by Fused-family, DYRK2 or MAP kinases, or when SUFU itself is ubiquitylated by SCF(FBXL17).

Molecular Function:
protein sequestering activity
Directly Involved In:
negative regulation of smoothened signaling pathway negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II negative regulation of protein import into nucleus
Cellular Locations:
cytoplasm nucleus
In Complex:
GLI-SUFU complex
Supporting Evidence:
  • PMID:10559945
    the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1, can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
  • PMID:24217340
    Mutations of critical interface residues disrupt the Sufu-Gli complex and prevent Sufu from repressing Gli-mediated transcription, tethering Gli in the cytoplasm and protecting Gli from the 26S proteasome-mediated degradation.
  • PMID:24311597
    Upon GLI binding, SUFU undergoes a large conformational change in which the N- and C-terminal domains come together to clamp highly conserved GLI residues
  • PMID:38968120
    Another negative regulator, SUFU, inhibits Hh signaling by retaining GLI2 and GLI3 in the cytoplasm and blocking their nuclear translocation.
  • file:human/SUFU/SUFU-notes.md
    three readouts of one binding event
  • file:human/SUFU/SUFU-notes.md
    Independent curator judgment therefore converges on the same

Adaptor activity that promotes conversion of GLI3 into its repressor form. In the pathway-off state SUFU bridges GLI3 to the enzymes that process it, recruiting GSK3B to GLI3 and linking GLI to the Slimb/BTRC-dependent degradation machinery, so that full-length GLI3 is efficiently cleaved into GLI3R. This happens on the SUFU-GLI3 complex delivered to the primary cilium, where the processing kinases act at the ciliary base. It is the one SUFU function separable from sequestration: here SUFU contributes positioning and recruitment to a proteolytic step carried out by other enzymes.

Molecular Function:
protein-macromolecule adaptor activity
Directly Involved In:
positive regulation of protein processing negative regulation of smoothened signaling pathway
Cellular Locations:
cilium ciliary base cytoplasm
Supporting Evidence:
  • PMID:10564661
    The data further suggest that Su(fu) can act by binding to Gli and inhibiting Gli-mediated transactivation as well as by serving as an adaptor protein, which links Gli to the Slimb-dependent proteasomal degradation pathway.
  • PMID:28965847
    Functional studies on cellular models and fibroblasts showed that both variants significantly reduced SUFU stability and its capacity to bind GLI3 and promote its cleavage into the repressor form GLI3R.

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Four kinases - STK36, ULK3, DYRK2 and ERK2 - plus S6K1 have each been shown to weaken the SUFU-GLI interface. Are these redundant, sequential, or cell-type-specific routes to the same release event, and which of them carries a normal Hedgehog response in a given human tissue?

Q: Is the DNA-bound corepressor role of SUFU, via the SAP18/SIN3 complex, a genuine human mechanism or an artefact of overexpression? The answer decides whether transcription corepressor activity is a secondary SUFU function or should be removed from the record.

Q: SUFU inhibits ULK3 kinase activity rather than being its substrate. Is inhibition of a Fused-family kinase a general SUFU activity that deserves its own molecular-function annotation, or is ULK3 a special case?

Q: Human SUFU has two abundant isoforms differing by a 52-residue C-terminal extension, with isoform 2 enriched in fetal testis. Do they differ in GLI affinity, ciliary recruitment, or susceptibility to SCF(FBXL17)? No GOA row is currently isoform-qualified.

Suggested Experiments

Experiment: Use the interface and IDR mutants already characterised structurally (for example the Tyr147/Asp159/Glu376 interface substitutions and the IDR deletion) in SUFU-null cells, and score sequestration (GLI1 nuclear/cytoplasmic ratio, co-immunoprecipitation) and GLI3R production (GLI3FL:GLI3R ratio by immunoblot) as independent readouts. A mutant that preserves binding but loses GSK3B recruitment would establish the adaptor role as a distinct molecular function rather than a consequence of holding GLI3.

Hypothesis: The GLI3-processing (adaptor) function of SUFU is genetically separable from its GLI-sequestering function.

Type: structure-guided mutagenesis with paired functional readouts

Experiment: In a single cell background, combinatorially deplete STK36, ULK3, DYRK2 and ERK2 and measure endogenous SUFU-GLI1, SUFU-GLI2 and SUFU-GLI3 complex occupancy by proximity ligation and co-immunoprecipitation after SMO agonist stimulation, alongside phospho-site specific antibodies for the known target serines. Redundancy predicts that no single depletion blocks release; specialisation predicts paralog-selective effects.

Hypothesis: The four kinase routes to SUFU-GLI dissociation act on different GLI paralogs or in different cell contexts rather than redundantly.

Type: combinatorial depletion with endogenous complex quantification

Experiment: Perform SUFU and GLI1 ChIP or CUT&RUN in cells with an intact Hedgehog response, and repeat in cells expressing a SUFU variant that cannot be ubiquitylated at Lys-257. If SUFU occupies GLI target promoters independently of its turnover, the transcription corepressor annotation is supported directly in human cells; if SUFU occupancy tracks only with GLI1 abundance, the corepressor reading should be dropped.

Hypothesis: The nuclear pool of SUFU acts on chromatin-bound GLI rather than simply being a staging compartment for SCF(FBXL17)-mediated turnover.

Type: chromatin occupancy profiling

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(SUFU-notes.md)

SUFU (Q9UMX1) — curation notes

Working journal for the GO annotation review of human SUFU (Suppressor of fused homolog,
HGNC:16466, UniProt Q9UMX1). Appended chronologically; every assertion carries provenance.

1. What the protein is

SUFU is a 484-residue (isoform 1, Su(fu)484) intracellular protein with no catalytic
domain, no transmembrane segment, and no recognisable enzymatic motif. It is the single
human member of the Suppressor of fused family (PANTHER PTHR10928; UniProt SIMILARITY:
"Belongs to the SUFU family"). It was cloned in parallel by two groups in 1999 as the
vertebrate homolog of Drosophila Su(fu)
PMID:10559945
and
PMID:10564661.
The two isoforms differ only by a 52-residue C-terminal extension
PMID:10564661,
and sequence identity to the fly protein is modest
PMID:10564661.

A striking asymmetry: the fly protein is dispensable, the mammalian one is essential.
PMID:24311597. This matters for the review: the
ancestral PAINT node assertion is about the mechanism (sequestering GLI/Ci), not about
the phenotypic weight of the gene in any one lineage.

2. Core mechanism — sequestration of GLI, not catalysis

The single best-supported statement about SUFU is that it binds full-length GLI1/GLI2/GLI3
and holds them out of productive transcriptional service. UniProt states this as the
FUNCTION line: "Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling pathway that acts
by sequestering the GLI (GI1, GLI2 and GLI3) transcription factors in the cytoplasm",
citing eleven primary papers.

The DYRK2 paper states the mechanism plainly:
PMID:38968120

The structural work defines the interface. Full-length human SUFU alternates between an
open and a closed state, clamping a short conserved GLI motif:
PMID:24311597
and the minimal recognition element is SYGHL
PMID:24311597.
The independent structure paper reaches the same two-state picture and, importantly,
shows that breaking the interface breaks all of SUFU's downstream effects at once:
PMID:24217340

That last sentence is the key curation fact. Transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic
tethering and protection from degradation are not three separate activities — they are
three readouts of one binding event. This is why GO:0140311 protein sequestering activity is the right core molecular function and the transcription-level terms are
consequences rather than independent functions.

An intrinsically disordered loop (IDR) in SUFU is the element through which upstream
signal is relayed:
PMID:24311597
Deleting it makes repression constitutive and SMO-agonist-resistant
PMID:24311597.

3. Second mechanism — promoting GLI3 processing to the repressor form

SUFU is not only a brake on GLI activator; it actively promotes conversion of full-length
GLI3 into the GLI3R repressor. UniProt: "In absence of smoothened signaling, the SUFU-GLI3
complex is recruited to cilia, leading to the efficient processing of full-length GLI3
into transcription repressor GLI3R: SUFU participates to GLI3 processing by promoting
recruitment of GSK3B to GLI3 (PubMed:24311597, PubMed:28965847)."

The Joubert-syndrome paper provides the human loss-of-function test:
PMID:28965847
with the pathway-level consequence
PMID:28965847

This is the evidence base for GO:0010954 positive regulation of protein processing and
for the adaptor-activity term GO:0030674 — SUFU physically links GLI to the kinases and
ubiquitin machinery that process it. The adaptor reading goes back to the original
characterisation:
PMID:10564661

4. How SUFU is released — the inputs

Several independent routes converge on weakening the SUFU–GLI interface. None of these are
SUFU activities; they are things done to SUFU or to its partner, and they matter here
only because the cited papers are the evidence for SUFU's own annotations.

  • Fused-family kinases. STK36 opposes SUFU
    (UniProt: "Interacts with STK36; promoting dissociation between SUFU and GLI (GI1, GLI2
    and GLI3) transcription factors (PubMed:10806483)"). The cached record for
    PMID:10806483 is title-only, so the UniProt statement is the usable provenance.
  • ULK3. A reciprocal-inhibition relationship, and the basis of the one
    GO:0019901 protein kinase binding annotation:
    PMID:20643644
    Note the direction: SUFU inhibits the kinase, not the other way round, and the
    Sufu-Ulk3 complex promotes GLI2 repressor formation
    PMID:20643644.
  • DYRK2 at the ciliary base.
    PMID:38968120
    with a direct affinity measurement
    PMID:38968120
  • ERK2/MAPK crosstalk.
    PMID:35831023
    quantitatively
    PMID:35831023.
  • mTOR/S6K1 crosstalk.
    PMID:22439934
  • SCF(FBXL17)-mediated turnover of SUFU itself.
    PMID:27234298
    UniProt localises the site: "Polyubiquitinated at Lys-257 by the SCF(FBXL17) complex".
    This is the one interaction row where SUFU is the substrate of an E3, which makes
    ubiquitin protein ligase binding the informative replacement for generic protein
    binding.

5. Where SUFU acts

Cytoplasm and nucleus are both experimentally established in human cells (UniProt
SUBCELLULAR LOCATION: "Cytoplasm {ECO:0000269|PubMed:10559945, ECO:0000269|PubMed:28965847}.
Nucleus {ECO:0000269|PubMed:10559945, ECO:0000269|PubMed:28965847}."). The nuclear pool is
not incidental — the original paper showed SUFU engages GLI1 on DNA as well as sequestering
it (PMID:10559945, quoted in §1), and FBXL17 degrades SUFU specifically in the nucleus
(PMID:27234298, §4).

Ciliary localisation is inferred by similarity in UniProt ("Cell projection, cilium
{ECO:0000250|UniProtKB:Q9Z0P7}") but is mechanistically load-bearing in the vertebrate
implementation of the pathway, and Reactome curates the trafficking explicitly
(R-HSA-5610766 "The intraflagellar transport A (IFT-A) complex is required for the transit
of GLI:SUFU complexes to the ciliary base"; R-HSA-5610767, the IFT-B counterpart to the
tip; R-HSA-5635860 "GLI:SUFU translocates to the ciliary tip in response to Hh signaling").
The DYRK2 work places the relevant phosphorylation at the ciliary base and observes SUFU
mislocalisation when the kinase is lost
PMID:38968120.
So cilium, ciliary base and ciliary tip are all defensible; the cytoplasmic/nuclear
pools remain the sites of the core activity.

6. Phylogeny — reading the IBA rows

The local PAINT slice interpro/panther/PTHR10928/PTHR10928-paint.tsv records node
PTN000859153 with IBD support for five terms:

term aspect seeds
GO:0005634 nucleus C fly Su(fu), mouse Sufu, human SUFU
GO:0005737 cytoplasm C fly Su(fu), mouse Sufu, human SUFU
GO:0140311 protein sequestering activity F mouse Sufu, human SUFU
GO:0000122 neg. reg. of Pol II transcription P mouse Sufu, human SUFU
GO:0045879 neg. reg. of smoothened signaling P fly Su(fu), mouse Sufu, zebrafish, human SUFU

Three points follow, and they are the ones easiest to get backwards:

  1. Human SUFU appearing in its own WITH/FROM is expected. SUFU has experimental
    annotations for nucleus, cytoplasm, GO:0140311 and GO:0045879; those experimental rows
    are among the descendant evidences the PAINT curator used to place the IBD. The IBA
    then says something additional — that the function is inherited from the eumetazoan
    node rather than being a vertebrate novelty. This is not circularity.
  2. The GO:0140311 node is seeded by only two descendants (mouse and human). A short
    donor list is not weak support: PTHR10928 is a small, single-copy family and the
    curator had the whole alignment in view.
  3. The node is placed at taxon:6072 (Eumetazoa), which is consistent with the module's
    framing of Hedgehog signaling as pan-metazoan. modules/hedgehog_signaling.yaml cites
    exactly this node for the sufu_sequestration annoton, with the same MF (GO:0140311),
    the same process (GO:0045879) and the same location (GO:0005737). Nothing in the
    literature reviewed here contradicts that annoton.

One divergence worth flagging: the local PAINT slice carries an IBD for GO:0000122 at
PTN000859153 (dated 2026-07-24), but the human GOA snapshot in SUFU-goa.tsv has no
corresponding IBA row for that term — human SUFU has GO:0000122 only by IDA, ARBA/IEA and
TAS. That is a snapshot-lag observation, not a defect in either file, and it does not
change any action.

7. GO-CAM grounding

gocams/index.tsv places SUFU in five curated human models (693b3c0900001389,
693b3c0900001501, 693b3c0900001575, 696022cd00001523, 696022cd00001645). Every one of
them types SUFU identically: MF GO:0140311 protein sequestering activity, BP
GO:0045879 negative regulation of smoothened signaling pathway, CC GO:0005737 cytoplasm. The models differ only in which Fused-family/DYRK kinase and which GLI
paralog they instantiate. Independent curator judgment therefore converges on the same
core triple that the PAINT node and the module use, which is a strong reason to treat
that triple as the core and everything else as consequence or context.

8. Judgments made in this review, and why

Transcription terms. GO:0000122 (negative regulation of Pol II transcription) is
ACCEPTed: SUFU binds the GLI transcription factor directly and the repression is a direct
consequence of that binding, demonstrated in reporter assays in the cited paper. By
contrast GO:0006355 regulation of DNA-templated transcription is the uninformative
parent — SUFU only ever regulates negatively, and only at Pol II promoters — so those
two rows are MODIFY → GO:0000122.

GO:0003714 transcription corepressor activity is kept but as non-core. It is not
wrong: SUFU is found on DNA with GLI1 (PMID:10559945) and UniProt records a DNA-bound
corepressor complex with SAP18 and SIN3 (by similarity to mouse). But both GOA rows are
TAS, the chromatin-corepressor mechanism is much less established than sequestration, and
the sequestering activity accounts for the repression readout on its own (PMID:24217340,
§2). Calling it core would put a secondary mechanism on the same footing as the one the
PAINT node, the module and all five GO-CAMs agree on.

GO:0007165 signal transduction. Both rows MODIFY → GO:0045879. This is not a case
of "IEA is allowed to be broad"; signal transduction is so general it carries no
information about a protein whose entire role is one specific negative step of one
specific pathway.

Rat-derived IEA rows. Two GO_REF:0000107 rows trace to the rat ortholog
(UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606). Tracing the donor annotations in QuickGO shows both rest on IEP
(expression-pattern) evidence:
- GO:0007286 spermatid development ← rat IEP from PMID:21893610, "Hedgehog signalling
promotes germ cell survival in the rat testis". Marked over-annotated: Hedgehog signaling
genuinely operates in the testis, so the term is not absurd, but an expression
correlation in rat does not establish that human SUFU executes a function in spermatid
development.
- GO:2001040 positive regulation of cellular response to drug ← rat IEP from
PMID:30790292, "Oroxylin A increases the sensitivity of temozolomide on glioma cells by
hypoxia-inducible factor 1α/hedgehog pathway under hypoxia". Removed: this is a
cell-line pharmacology observation, propagated first as an expression-pattern annotation
in rat and then electronically into human. It asserts a physiological function that no
evidence supports.

GO:0008013 beta-catenin binding. Kept as non-core. UniProt records the CTNNB1
complex and the beta-catenin-regulation role only "By similarity" from mouse, and it sits
outside the Hedgehog axis that defines the protein.

Generic protein binding (74 rows). Per repository policy these are never ACCEPTed.
Thirteen rows whose partner is a GLI transcription factor (GLI1 P08151, GLI3 P10071, mouse
Gli2 Q0VGT2) become MODIFY → GO:0140297 DNA-binding transcription factor binding: GLIs
are sequence-specific zinc-finger transcription factors, the interaction is the single
most replicated fact about SUFU, and the replacement term states something the bare term
does not. Three kinase-partner rows become informative MF terms — STK36 (×2) →
GO:0019901 protein kinase binding, and FBXL17 in the mechanistic EMBO J study →
GO:0031625 ubiquitin protein ligase binding, the standard GO treatment of a substrate
that engages an SCF complex. The remaining ~58 rows come from large-scale interactome
surveys (PMID:16189514, 20211142, 25416956, 28514442, 31403225, 31515488, 32296183,
32814053, 33961781, 35140242, 40205054) and name partners with no established connection
to SUFU biology — keratins, ZNF/KRAB proteins, RCN3, PEX26, STX19, SLC41A3 and so on.
Those are REMOVE: the measurements may well be real, but a bare protein binding row
carries no functional information, and inventing a specific function from a single Y2H or
AP-MS hit is exactly what the guidelines forbid. One further row, BRAF from
PMID:35512704 ("Systematic discovery of mutation-directed neo-protein-protein interactions
in cancer"), is a neo-interaction of a mutant protein; it is removed for the same reason
and would not justify a kinase-binding term even if kept.

No NEW annotations proposed. The three things one might be tempted to add all fail
the tests in CLAUDE.md. GO:0007224 smoothened signaling pathway is an ancestor of
GO:0045879, which SUFU already carries eight times — redundancy, not coverage.
GO:0140416 transcription regulator inhibitor activity is tempting, but SUFU's inhibition
of GLI is already expressed by GO:0140311 plus GO:0000122, and adding it would assert a
second molecular function where the structural work says there is one binding event with
several readouts (PMID:24217340, §2). Terms for the developmental phenotypes of SUFU loss
(neural tube, limb, cerebellum) describe outcomes of deregulated GLI activity in other
cells and tissues, not steps SUFU performs.

9. Open questions carried into the review

  • Which of the four documented release mechanisms (STK36, ULK3, DYRK2, ERK2/S6K1) dominates
    in a normal Hedgehog response in a given human tissue, and are they redundant or
    sequential? PMID:38968120 explicitly leaves this open ("it is plausible that other sites
    must undergo PTMs in a coordinated and sequential manner to achieve complete SUFU
    dissociation").
  • Is the DNA-bound corepressor role (SAP18/SIN3) a genuine human mechanism or a
    mouse-specific/overexpression artefact? It is the difference between GO:0003714 being
    non-core and being wrong.
  • The isoforms: Q9UMX1-1 (484 aa) is the major isoform and Q9UMX1-2 (433 aa) is
    enriched in fetal testis (PMID:10564661). Both bind GLI1 in IntAct. No GOA row is
    isoform-qualified, so no isoform field was added.

📄 View Raw YAML

 
id: Q9UMX1
gene_symbol: SUFU
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: 'SUFU (Suppressor of fused homolog) is a 484-residue intracellular protein with no catalytic
  domain and no enzymatic activity, and the single human member of the Suppressor of fused family. It
  is the principal negative regulator of the vertebrate Hedgehog pathway acting downstream of Smoothened.
  SUFU binds full-length GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 zinc-finger transcription factors through a conserved interface
  that closes around the SYGHL motif of GLI, and this single interaction accounts for its several documented
  effects: GLI is held in the cytoplasm and blocked from entering the nucleus, its transcriptional activity
  is repressed even where the two are bound together on DNA, and it is protected from proteasomal degradation.
  In the pathway-off state SUFU additionally acts as an adaptor that promotes proteolytic conversion of
  full-length GLI3 into the GLI3R repressor by recruiting GSK3B to GLI3; the SUFU-GLI3 complex is delivered
  to the primary cilium, and intraflagellar transport moves it between the ciliary base, where the processing
  kinases act, and the ciliary tip. Hedgehog signalling releases GLI from SUFU: the Fused-family kinases
  STK36 and ULK3, the ciliary kinase DYRK2, ERK/MAPK and S6K1 each weaken the SUFU-GLI interface by phosphorylating
  GLI, and SCF(FBXL17) ubiquitylates SUFU itself at Lys-257 to drive its turnover in the nucleus. SUFU
  is expressed ubiquitously in adult tissues and is found in the cytoplasm, the nucleoplasm and the cilium.
  Loss of SUFU raises Hedgehog output: knockout is embryonic-lethal in mouse, germline loss-of-function
  in humans predisposes to medulloblastoma and causes basal cell nevus syndrome, and hypomorphic recessive
  missense variants cause Joubert syndrome with cranio-facial and skeletal defects.'
alternative_products:
