- name: 2 (Su(fu)433)
  id: Q9UMX1-2
  sequence_note: VSP_013278, VSP_013279
- name: '3'
  id: Q9UMX1-3
  sequence_note: VSP_013280
existing_annotations:
- term:
    id: GO:0000122
    label: negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
  evidence_type: IDA
  original_reference_id: PMID:10564661
  qualifier: acts_upstream_of_or_within
  review:
    summary: Stone et al. showed that human SUFU represses the transcriptional activity of GLI in reporter
      assays while binding GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 directly. Because SUFU acts on the transcription factor
      itself rather than on an upstream input, repression of GLI target genes is an immediate consequence
      of SUFU binding.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: Accepted as a core process. GLI1/2/3 are RNA polymerase II transcription factors, and SUFU's
      only established biochemical activity is to bind them and hold them inactive, so this term names
      something SUFU genuinely executes rather than a distant downstream phenotype. The same term carries
      an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node PTN000859153 in the local PAINT slice, placing the repression
      function deep in Eumetazoa. (The human GOA snapshot does not yet carry a matching IBA row for GO:0000122;
      that is snapshot lag between the PAINT slice and the GOA release, not a disagreement, and it changes
      nothing here.)
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10564661
      supporting_text: HSu(fu) was found to repress activity of the zinc-finger transcription factor Gli,
        which mediates Hedgehog signaling in vertebrates, and to physically interact with Gli, Gli2 and
        Gli3
    - reference_id: PMID:10564661
      supporting_text: our data provide biochemical and functional evidence for the hypothesis that Su(fu)
        is a key negative regulator in the vertebrate Hedgehog signaling pathway
- term:
    id: GO:0000122
    label: negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
  evidence_type: IEA
  original_reference_id: GO_REF:0000117
  qualifier: involved_in
  supporting_entities:
  - ARBA:ARBA00029133
  review:
    summary: An ARBA machine-learning rule (ARBA00029133) assigns negative regulation of Pol II transcription
      to SUFU on the basis of its UniProt family and functional annotation.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: Accepted. The electronic assignment lands on exactly the term that the human IDA row and the
      PAINT node independently support, so the rule is correct for this protein rather than being a broad
      default. Where an ARBA rule is too generic for SUFU it has been modified instead (see GO:0007165),
      so this is not blanket acceptance of the ARBA source.
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling
    propagation_review:
      root_cause: NO_FAILURE_CORE
      source_entities:
      - source_id: ARBA:ARBA00029133
        source_label: null
        source_status: SUPPORTS_TRANSFER
        comment: Rule output coincides with the human IDA annotation and with the PTHR10928 IBD node,
          so the electronic call is independently corroborated.
- term:
    id: GO:0000122
    label: negative regulation of transcription by RNA polymerase II
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: PMID:11001584
  qualifier: involved_in
  review:
    summary: A GOA row taken from the Villavicencio et al. review of the sonic hedgehog-patched-gli pathway
      in human development and disease, asserting that SUFU negatively regulates GLI-driven transcription.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: 'Accepted. The cached record for this review contains citation metadata only, with no abstract
      or full text, so the author statement behind the TAS cannot be quoted here and no supporting_text
      is offered. The assertion itself is not in doubt: it duplicates a claim that the IDA row from PMID:10564661
      establishes experimentally in human cells and that the ancestral PAINT node supports phylogenetically.
      A TAS row that restates a well-evidenced term is redundant rather than wrong, and UNDECIDED would
      misrepresent the state of the evidence for this term.'
- term:
    id: GO:0003714
    label: transcription corepressor activity
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: PMID:10559945
  qualifier: enables
  review:
    summary: Kogerman et al. showed that SUFU sequesters GLI1 in the cytoplasm but can also interact with
      GLI1 while it is on DNA, which is the observation behind reading SUFU as a transcriptional corepressor
      rather than purely a cytoplasmic anchor.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: 'Kept, but marked non-core. It is not wrong: SUFU is found with GLI1 on DNA, and UniProt records
      a DNA-bound corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3. But two things keep it off the
      core list. First, both GOA rows are TAS and the corepressor complex itself is annotated in UniProt
      only By similarity from mouse, so the human evidence is thinner than for sequestration. Second,
      and more decisively, the repression readout is already accounted for by the sequestering activity:
      Zhang et al. showed that mutating the single SUFU-GLI interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional
      repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which means these
      are three readouts of one binding event rather than three activities. Promoting GO:0003714 to core
      would put a less-established mechanism on the same footing as the one that the PAINT node, the hedgehog_signaling
      module and all five curated GO-CAM models agree on.'
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10559945
      supporting_text: the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1,
        can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
    - reference_id: PMID:24217340
      supporting_text: Mutations of critical interface residues disrupt the Sufu-Gli complex and prevent
        Sufu from repressing Gli-mediated transcription, tethering Gli in the cytoplasm and protecting
        Gli from the 26S proteasome-mediated degradation.
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3 (By similarity).
- term:
    id: GO:0003714
    label: transcription corepressor activity
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: PMID:10564661
  qualifier: enables
  review:
    summary: Stone et al. reported that SUFU represses GLI-mediated transactivation while bound to GLI,
      and that repression is potentiated by co-expression of Slimb, consistent with SUFU acting on the
      transcription factor at the promoter as well as in the cytoplasm.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: 'Kept, but marked non-core. It is not wrong: SUFU is found with GLI1 on DNA, and UniProt records
      a DNA-bound corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3. But two things keep it off the
      core list. First, both GOA rows are TAS and the corepressor complex itself is annotated in UniProt
      only By similarity from mouse, so the human evidence is thinner than for sequestration. Second,
      and more decisively, the repression readout is already accounted for by the sequestering activity:
      Zhang et al. showed that mutating the single SUFU-GLI interface simultaneously abolishes transcriptional
      repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which means these
      are three readouts of one binding event rather than three activities. Promoting GO:0003714 to core
      would put a less-established mechanism on the same footing as the one that the PAINT node, the hedgehog_signaling
      module and all five curated GO-CAM models agree on.'
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10564661
      supporting_text: The data further suggest that Su(fu) can act by binding to Gli and inhibiting Gli-mediated
        transactivation as well as by serving as an adaptor protein, which links Gli to the Slimb-dependent
        proteasomal degradation pathway.
    - reference_id: PMID:10564661
      supporting_text: Coexpression of Slimb with Su(fu) potentiated the Su(fu)-mediated repression of
        Gli.
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: corepressor complex containing SAP18, GLI1 and SIN3 (By similarity).
- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:10564661
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P10071
  review:
    summary: 'GOA records this physical-interaction row from the original characterisation of human SUFU,
      with GLI3 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical
      fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct
      records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface.
      The bare term records none of that.'
    action: MODIFY
    reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather
      than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific
      zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely
      what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout
      follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation
      are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term,
      so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.
    proposed_replacement_terms: &id001
    - id: GO:0140297
      label: DNA-binding transcription factor binding
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: transcription factors in the cytoplasm
- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:10806483
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q9NRP7
  review:
    summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from the study of the opposing activities of Fused
      and Suppressor of fused with the Fused-family kinase STK36 as the partner. STK36 is the vertebrate
      orthologue of Drosophila Fused and acts on the SUFU-GLI complex to release GLI, so the interaction
      has a defined mechanistic role rather than being an isolated detection.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: 'Generic protein binding is uninformative, but here the partner class is itself the information:
      STK36 is a serine/threonine protein kinase and UniProt records the interaction together with its
      functional consequence. Protein kinase binding is the evidence-backed replacement, and SUFU already
      carries that term by IPI for the related kinase ULK3 (GO:0019901, PMID:20643644), so the replacement
      makes the record internally consistent rather than introducing a new claim.'
    proposed_replacement_terms: &id002
    - id: GO:0019901
      label: protein kinase binding
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: with STK36; promoting dissociation between SUFU and GLI
- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:16189514
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q7Z412
  review:
    summary: GOA records this physical-interaction row from a proteome-scale yeast two-hybrid map; the
      reported partner is PEX26. The row asserts only that the two proteins were co-detected, and says
      nothing about what SUFU does.
    action: REMOVE
    reason: Removed because the generic term carries no functional information, not because the measurement
      is doubted. PEX26 is not a documented participant in any SUFU mechanism recorded in the UniProt
      FUNCTION or SUBUNIT annotations, nor in the Hedgehog literature reviewed here, so there is no evidence-backed
      molecular function to promote the row to; inventing one from a single large-scale detection is exactly
      what the curation guidelines forbid. The interaction itself remains available in IntAct and in the
      UniProt INTERACTION block, where it is a better fit than a GO molecular-function assertion.
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-notes.md
      supporting_text: a bare `protein binding` row

    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:24035498
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q9UF56
  review:
    summary: GOA records this row from a parallel SCF-adaptor capture proteomics screen, with FBXL17 as
      the partner. That study is a systematic survey of F-box protein interactors whose validation went
      to BACH1 and the NRF2 axis; SUFU appears as one candidate among many.

    reason: Removed as uninformative. The SUFU-FBXL17 relationship is real and is retained through the
      companion row anchored to PMID:27234298, which established it mechanistically and is modified here
      to ubiquitin protein ligase binding. This screening row would add a second bare protein-binding
      assertion for the same pair without adding functional content.

  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:24311597
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P08151
  review:
    summary: 'GOA records this physical-interaction row from the human SUFU-GLI co-structure study, with
      GLI1 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated biochemical fact
      about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function, IntAct records
      27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define the interface.
      The bare term records none of that.'
    action: MODIFY
    reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather
      than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific
      zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely
      what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout
      follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation
      are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term,
      so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.

- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:35140242
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P10071
  review:
    summary: 'GOA records this physical-interaction row from a human transcription-factor interaction
      network, with GLI3 as the reported partner. SUFU-GLI association is the most heavily replicated
      biochemical fact about this protein: UniProt cites eleven primary papers for the sequestering function,
      IntAct records 27 experiments for the GLI1 pair alone, and two independent co-structures define
      the interface. The bare term records none of that.'
    action: MODIFY
    reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative and is not kept, but the row should be promoted rather
      than discarded, because the cited evidence supports a specific term. GLI1, GLI2 and GLI3 are sequence-specific
      zinc-finger DNA-binding transcription factors, so DNA-binding transcription factor binding is precisely
      what this IPI row demonstrates, and it is the binding event from which every downstream SUFU readout
      follows - cytoplasmic tethering, transcriptional repression and protection from proteasomal degradation
      are three consequences of one interaction, not three activities. GO has no GLI-specific child term,
      so GO:0140297 is the most specific defensible replacement.
    proposed_replacement_terms: *id001
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: Negative regulator in the hedgehog/smoothened signaling
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: transcription factors in the cytoplasm

  review:
    summary: GOA records this row from a screen for mutation-directed neo-interactions in cancer, with

      proteins rather than interactions of the wild-type pair.
    action: REMOVE
    reason: Removed as uninformative, and the experimental design additionally blocks promotion to a specific

- term:
    id: GO:0005634
    label: nucleus
  evidence_type: EXP
  original_reference_id: PMID:10559945
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: The original human SUFU paper localised the protein and showed that it modulates the nuclear-cytoplasmic
      distribution of GLI1.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: 'Accepted as a functionally relevant location rather than as incidental presence. The nuclear
      pool does work: SUFU engages GLI1 on DNA (PMID:10559945), and SCF(FBXL17) degrades SUFU specifically
      in the nucleus to release GLI1 (PMID:27234298), so nuclear SUFU is both an actor and a regulated
      target there. The shuttling behaviour is the mechanism by which SUFU controls where GLI is allowed
      to accumulate.'
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10559945
      supporting_text: may affect nuclear-cytoplasmic shuttling of GLI-1 or the activity of GLI-1 in the
        nucleus
    - reference_id: PMID:10559945
      supporting_text: the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1,
        can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA

- term:
    id: GO:0006355
    label: regulation of DNA-templated transcription
  evidence_type: IDA
  original_reference_id: PMID:24311597
  qualifier: involved_in
  review:
    summary: Cherry et al. measured GLI1-driven reporter transcription in HEK 293 cells transfected with
      wild-type and interface-mutant SUFU constructs, showing that SUFU represses GLI activity and that
      specific interface residues are required for it.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: The essence is right but the term is the uninformative parent. SUFU regulates transcription
      in one direction only - it represses - and only at RNA polymerase II promoters driven by GLI transcription
      factors. There is no evidence anywhere in this record for SUFU raising transcription of anything,
      so the neutral parent term understates what is known. The child term proposed here is already carried
      by SUFU with IDA, IEA and TAS evidence and is supported by an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node,
      so this is a demotion of a redundant parent rather than a new claim.

        with mutated SUFU constructs

        to repress GLI, whereas the mutation of Glu376 and His164 had a smaller effect.

      of the hedgehog_signaling module; and all five curated human GO-CAM models that contain SUFU. Nothing
      in the literature reviewed here contradicts it. As a node assertion this IBA says something the
      experimental rows do not: that negative regulation of Smoothened signalling is ancestral within
      the family rather than a vertebrate innovation. That is a meaningful additional claim given that
      the fly protein is dispensable for embryogenesis while the mammalian one is essential - the difference
      is in phenotypic weight, not in mechanism. Human SUFU in the WITH/FROM is expected: its own experimental
      annotations are among the descendant evidences behind the IBD.'

      supporting_text: then says something *additional*

- term:
    id: GO:0097542
    label: ciliary tip
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: Reactome:R-HSA-5610766
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: Reactome places SUFU at the ciliary tip as a participant in the curated reaction "The intraflagellar
      transport A (IFT-A) complex is required for the transit of GLI:SUFU complexes to the ciliary base".
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: Accepted. In the vertebrate implementation of this pathway the GLI:SUFU complex is carried
      by anterograde intraflagellar transport to the ciliary tip on pathway activation, and it is there
      that the complex disassembles and GLI is released. Reactome curates these as explicit reactions
      with SUFU as a participant, and UniProt records that SUFU is recruited to cilia in response to Smoothened
      signalling, leading to dissociation from GLI. The location is therefore where a discrete step of
      the core mechanism happens, not incidental presence.
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: Recruited to cilia in response to
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: smoothened signaling, the SUFU-GLI3 complex is recruited to cilia,

    id: GO:0097546
    label: ciliary base
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: Reactome:R-HSA-5610720
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: Reactome places SUFU at the ciliary base as a participant in the curated reaction "PKA phosphorylates
      GLI3".
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: 'Accepted. The ciliary base is where the pathway-off processing cassette operates on the GLI:SUFU
      complex: retrograde IFT-A transport returns the complex there, the PKA/CK1/GSK3 phosphorylation
      and beta-TrCP-dependent partial proteolysis that generate GLI3R are curated at that location, and
      SUFU is a participant in each of those reactions because it is what holds GLI while the kinases
      act. Independent work places the SMO-dependent DYRK2 phosphorylation of GLI2/GLI3 at the ciliary
      base as well. This is the compartment in which SUFU''s adaptor contribution to GLI3 processing is
      executed.'

      supporting_text: promoting recruitment of GSK3B to GLI3

      supporting_text: leading to the efficient processing of full-length GLI3 into

      SUFU is a participant in each of those reactions because it is what holds GLI while the kinases
      act. Independent work places the SMO-dependent DYRK2 phosphorylation of GLI2/GLI3 at the ciliary
      base as well. This is the compartment in which SUFU''s adaptor contribution to GLI3 processing is
      executed.'
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: promoting recruitment of GSK3B to GLI3
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: leading to the efficient processing of full-length GLI3 into

    reason: 'Accepted. This is the correct refinement of the parent cilium annotation: Hedgehog signalling

- term:
    id: GO:0140311
    label: protein sequestering activity
  evidence_type: IDA
  original_reference_id: PMID:10559945
  qualifier: enables
  review:
    summary: Kogerman et al. showed that human SUFU binds GLI1 and can sequester it in the cytoplasm,
      the observation that named the activity.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: Accepted as the core molecular function. SUFU has no catalytic domain and no enzymatic activity;
      what it does is bind full-length GLI1/GLI2/GLI3 through a defined interface and hold them out of
      productive service. Zhang et al. showed that disrupting that single interface simultaneously abolishes
      transcriptional repression, cytoplasmic tethering and protection from proteasomal degradation, which
      establishes that these are three readouts of one sequestering event rather than three separate activities.
      The term carries an IBD at the PTHR10928 ancestral node, is the molecular function of the sufu_sequestration
      annoton in the hedgehog_signaling module, and is the molecular function assigned to SUFU in all
      five curated human GO-CAM models.
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:10559945
      supporting_text: the gene product interacts physically with the transcriptional effector GLI-1,
        can sequester GLI-1 in the cytoplasm, but can also interact with GLI-1 on DNA
    - reference_id: PMID:24217340
      supporting_text: Mutations of critical interface residues disrupt the Sufu-Gli complex and prevent
        Sufu from repressing Gli-mediated transcription, tethering Gli in the cytoplasm and protecting
        Gli from the 26S proteasome-mediated degradation.
    additional_reference_ids:
    - PMID:24217340

  evidence_type: IEA
  original_reference_id: GO_REF:0000107

  - ensembl:ENSRNOP00000087532

    summary: An Ensembl Compara projection (GO_REF:0000107) onto human SUFU from the rat orthologue Sufu
      (UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606).
    action: REMOVE
    reason: 'Removed. Tracing the donor shows the rat annotation is an IEP - inferred from expression
      pattern - made from PMID:30790292, a study reporting that Oroxylin A increases the sensitivity of
      glioma cells to temozolomide via an HIF-1alpha/Hedgehog axis under hypoxia. That is a cell-line
      pharmacology observation, not a physiological function, and it has been propagated twice: first
      into a process annotation on the rat gene on expression evidence alone, then electronically into
      human. Nothing in the human record - no experimental row, no PAINT node term, no GO-CAM model, no
      UniProt statement - supports human SUFU having a role in drug response. This is distinct from the
      spermatid-development row, which is flagged rather than removed because a testis role is at least
      biologically coherent for a Hedgehog regulator; a positive role in cellular drug response is not
      a function this protein can be said to have.'
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/SUFU/SUFU-notes.md
      supporting_text: Tracing the donor annotations in QuickGO shows both rest on **IEP**
    propagation_review:
      root_cause: SOURCE_WEAK_OR_INFERRED
      failure_modes:
      - SOURCE_EVIDENCE_WEAK
      - CONTEXT_OR_TISSUE_MISMATCH
      source_entities:
      - source_id: UniProtKB:A0A8I6A606

- id: Reactome:R-NUL-5610751
  title: Gli2is degraded by the proteasome
  findings: []
core_functions:
- description: 'Sequestration of the GLI transcription factors. SUFU binds full-length GLI1, GLI2 and
    GLI3 through a bipartite interface that clamps the conserved SYGHL motif, holding them in the cytoplasm,
    blocking their nuclear import, repressing their transcriptional output and shielding them from proteasomal
    degradation. These are readouts of one binding event: mutating the interface abolishes all of them
    together. The activity is relieved when GLI is phosphorylated by Fused-family, DYRK2 or MAP kinases,
    or when SUFU itself is ubiquitylated by SCF(FBXL17).'
  molecular_function:

  - reference_id: PMID:38968120

    supporting_text: Independent curator judgment therefore converges on the same
- description: 'Adaptor activity that promotes conversion of GLI3 into its repressor form. In the pathway-off
    state SUFU bridges GLI3 to the enzymes that process it, recruiting GSK3B to GLI3 and linking GLI to
    the Slimb/BTRC-dependent degradation machinery, so that full-length GLI3 is efficiently cleaved into
    GLI3R. This happens on the SUFU-GLI3 complex delivered to the primary cilium, where the processing
    kinases act at the ciliary base. It is the one SUFU function separable from sequestration: here SUFU
    contributes positioning and recruitment to a proteolytic step carried out by other enzymes.'
  molecular_function:

proposed_new_terms: []
suggested_questions:
- question: Four kinases - STK36, ULK3, DYRK2 and ERK2 - plus S6K1 have each been shown to weaken the
    SUFU-GLI interface. Are these redundant, sequential, or cell-type-specific routes to the same release
    event, and which of them carries a normal Hedgehog response in a given human tissue?
  experts: []
- question: Is the DNA-bound corepressor role of SUFU, via the SAP18/SIN3 complex, a genuine human mechanism
    or an artefact of overexpression? The answer decides whether transcription corepressor activity is
    a secondary SUFU function or should be removed from the record.
  experts: []
- question: SUFU inhibits ULK3 kinase activity rather than being its substrate. Is inhibition of a Fused-family
    kinase a general SUFU activity that deserves its own molecular-function annotation, or is ULK3 a special
    case?
  experts: []
- question: Human SUFU has two abundant isoforms differing by a 52-residue C-terminal extension, with
    isoform 2 enriched in fetal testis. Do they differ in GLI affinity, ciliary recruitment, or susceptibility
    to SCF(FBXL17)? No GOA row is currently isoform-qualified.
  experts: []
suggested_experiments:
- hypothesis: The GLI3-processing (adaptor) function of SUFU is genetically separable from its GLI-sequestering
    function.
  description: Use the interface and IDR mutants already characterised structurally (for example the Tyr147/Asp159/Glu376
    interface substitutions and the IDR deletion) in SUFU-null cells, and score sequestration (GLI1 nuclear/cytoplasmic
    ratio, co-immunoprecipitation) and GLI3R production (GLI3FL:GLI3R ratio by immunoblot) as independent
    readouts. A mutant that preserves binding but loses GSK3B recruitment would establish the adaptor
    role as a distinct molecular function rather than a consequence of holding GLI3.
  experiment_type: structure-guided mutagenesis with paired functional readouts
- hypothesis: The four kinase routes to SUFU-GLI dissociation act on different GLI paralogs or in different
    cell contexts rather than redundantly.
  description: In a single cell background, combinatorially deplete STK36, ULK3, DYRK2 and ERK2 and measure
    endogenous SUFU-GLI1, SUFU-GLI2 and SUFU-GLI3 complex occupancy by proximity ligation and co-immunoprecipitation
    after SMO agonist stimulation, alongside phospho-site specific antibodies for the known target serines.
    Redundancy predicts that no single depletion blocks release; specialisation predicts paralog-selective
    effects.
  experiment_type: combinatorial depletion with endogenous complex quantification
- hypothesis: The nuclear pool of SUFU acts on chromatin-bound GLI rather than simply being a staging
    compartment for SCF(FBXL17)-mediated turnover.
  description: Perform SUFU and GLI1 ChIP or CUT&RUN in cells with an intact Hedgehog response, and repeat
    in cells expressing a SUFU variant that cannot be ubiquitylated at Lys-257. If SUFU occupies GLI target
    promoters independently of its turnover, the transcription corepressor annotation is supported directly
    in human cells; if SUFU occupancy tracks only with GLI1 abundance, the corepressor reading should
    be dropped.
  experiment_type: chromatin occupancy profiling
